In bacteria (particularly E. coli and other enteric bacteria), sulfate transporters like the CysPUWA operon are regulated by sulfur availability:
- The cysteine biosynthesis regulon (including sulfate transporters) is induced under sulfur-limiting conditions
- The regulatory mechanism involves CysB (a transcriptional activator) and O-acetyl-L-serine (OAS), which is a co-inducer
- When sulfur is abundant (cysteine is available), the pathway is repressed
- When sulfur is scarce, OAS accumulates (because it can&#x27;t be converted to cysteine), and OAS + CysB activate transcription of the cysteine regulon genes

So the regulation pattern is:
- Expression increases during sulfur starvation

- Repressed by cysteine (or when cysteine/sulfur is abundant)
